## `StartIndicator`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StartIndicator.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct StartIndicator
```

The camera's first start (docs/design/LAUNCH-SCREEN.md, 6.3): the launch screen's mark at the
same point, until the picture arrives.

``CameraScreen`` shows it over the whole screen while `CameraModel.isFirstStart` is `true`
and fades it out with ``NopeMotion/fade`` when the first start ends, so the app's first
frames are the launch screen and the mark is part of the first screen, as Apple's Human
Interface Guidelines ("Launching") ask of a launch screen with a mark.

The mark is ``NopeSize/startMark`` square with its center at the center of the window, not of
the safe area or the picture, where the launch screen draws its image
(`UIImageRespectsSafeAreaInsets` off). While the camera is ready and starting, "Starting the
camera…" is centered under it, its top edge ``wordsTop`` under the mark's center, on a plate of
``NopeMaterial/solid`` that can't be seen on the black picture and keeps the words readable over
the scrolling layout's rows. Before the camera state is known there are no words and no plate:
that frame is the launch screen again. It turns with
``SwiftUICore/EnvironmentValues/cameraUprightRotation`` about the mark's center, so it is
upright on an iPhone held sideways, as the launch screen was. It takes no touches, and
VoiceOver reads it as one element, "Starting the camera…".

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StartIndicator.swift`

```swift

```

The mark at the window's center, with the words under it.


## `StartMark`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StartIndicator.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct StartMark
```

The launch screen's mark: the app icon's frame, "n" and period without the icon's ground
(docs/design/LAUNCH-SCREEN.md, 5 and 6.3).

The shapes are the three filled paths of `App/NopeApp/AppIcon.icon/Assets` (frame.svg, n.svg
and period.svg), on their 1024-unit grid, which are also the paths of the launch image
(`LaunchMark.svg`, a copy of docs/design/launch-mark.svg); `scripts/check-launch-screen.sh`
checks the files agree. They are the outlines of the icon's 8-unit square-cap strokes on its
128-unit grid (docs/design/icon.html), so the mark drawn here and the launch image cover the
same pixels. Shapes, not text: no font (AGENTS.md, "Name and wordmark").

The colors are fixed, as the launch image's are: ``NopeColor/mark`` and
``NopeColor/markPeriod``, not ``NopeColor/label`` or ``NopeColor/fact``, which is the system
teal and changes with Increase Contrast. In night mode both are white, so `NightModeStyle`'s
multiply turns the whole mark the night red; the teal would multiply to a red too dark to
see. Hidden from VoiceOver: ``StartIndicator`` carries the label.

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StartIndicator.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var body: some View { get }
```

The frame and "n" in ``NopeColor/mark`` and the period in ``NopeColor/markPeriod``, or all
white in night mode.

## `StatePill`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StatePills.swift`

```swift
struct StatePill
```

One small pill on the status line for a setting that isn't at its default, such as the light's
brightness or a lock (issue #68, "Non-default state"). Defaults are invisible.

A pill has a symbol and short text, so it never relies on color, and one or more actions
to change or reset it: one action makes the pill a button, more make it a menu.

## `StatePills`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StatePills.swift`

```swift
@MainActor enum StatePills
```

The registry of state pills: each feature with a non-default state adds one function here
that returns its pill, or `nil` at the default.

No pill repeats what a control on the camera already shows (docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.3):
manual controls have the Manual Controls button, and the drive modes and Astro have the drive
strip, which also turn them off.

The order of ``builders`` is the order on screen; ``StatePillRow`` shows the first
``StatePillRow/maximumVisible`` and folds the rest into "+n".

## `StatusMessageView`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/StatusMessageView.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct StatusMessageView<Actions> where Actions : View
```

A centered message with a symbol, a title, an explanation and optional actions
(docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.8, figures `camera-denied` and `camera-unavailable`).

Used for the permission and no-camera states. It is built from plain `Text`, `Image` and
`Button` views rather than `ContentUnavailableView`, because that view's symbol keeps a fixed
size and fails the accessibility audit's Dynamic Type check. At large text sizes the content
scrolls instead of being clipped (AGENTS.md, "Larger Text"). The actions are pinned below it,
full width, in the primary style (``NopePrimaryActionStyle``), so the one action is always in
reach.

## `ThumbnailSlot`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CaptureFeedback.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct ThumbnailSlot
```

The last-photo slot beside the shutter (issue #68, "Feedback tiers"): empty and invisible
until the first photo of the session, never an empty square. Then it shows the last photo with
a teal seal when every file was signed, or an amber mark when signing was on and a file
couldn't be signed. With signing off by choice it has no badge (docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.3
and 3.7): that isn't a warning, and its value still says "Saved. Not signed: signing is off in
Settings."

With ``image``, it shows the photo itself (issue #36), made in memory from the saved file;
without one, a photo symbol. With ``openViewer`` it is a button: a tap opens the Last Photo
viewer (docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.14). VoiceOver and Voice Control also offer Open in Photos
and Photo Details as actions, so both are one action away without opening the viewer; there
is no touch-and-hold menu (decision V3).

## `UprightRotation`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
enum UprightRotation
```

Which way up the person holds an iPhone whose camera layout stays in portrait, so icons and
text blocks can turn to read upright (issue #68, "Landscape (iPhone)").

The app target sets it from the device's orientation with ``SwiftUICore/EnvironmentValues/cameraUprightRotation``.
On iPad the interface itself rotates, so the app leaves it at ``portrait``.

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
func inWindow(isRegularSize: Bool) -> UprightRotation
```

The turn upright content needs in a window, given the device's orientation: none in a
window that is regular in both size classes (``CameraLayout/isRegularSize(horizontal:vertical:)``),
because that window rotates with the device, as on iPad and on the unfolded inner display
of a foldable iPhone, where the system turns the interface whatever the app asks.

- Parameter isRegularSize: Whether the window is regular in both size classes.
- Returns: This rotation in a portrait-only window, otherwise ``portrait``.

## `UprightUnit`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct UprightUnit
```

Turns a block of content upright as one unit while the iPhone is on its side, and gives it
the turned size in the layout, so neighbors make room for it.

It measures the content's natural size, turns it a quarter turn, and swaps the frame's width
and height. With `sidewaysWidth`, text wraps at that width before turning, for banners.

The measured size settles (``settledSize(_:measured:)``): it is rounded up to whole points,
and within a point of the size in use it only grows. The turned frame can sit at a
fractional position, where the content measured a third of a point larger or smaller; taking
every such measurement made the frame move, the content measure again, and so on without end,
which kept the main thread busy (a sideways iPhone at the largest text size with manual controls on; UI test
testEveryManualControlIsSettableInLandscapeAtTheLargestTextSize).

## `UprightUnit/body(content:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func body(content: Content) -> some View

```

The content, turned when the device is on its side.

- Parameter content: The content.

## `UprightUnit/init(sidewaysWidth:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor init(sidewaysWidth: CGFloat?)
```

Creates the modifier.

- Parameter sidewaysWidth: The width the content wraps at while sideways, or `nil` for
  its natural width.

## `UprightUnit/settledSize(_:measured:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor static func settledSize(_ current: CGSize, measured: CGSize) -> CGSize
```

The size to lay out with, given the size in use and a new measurement: the measurement
rounded up to whole points, so the frame is never smaller than the content. Within a point
of the size in use, in both dimensions, the size only grows, so sub-point changes can't
make it go back and forth; a larger change is taken as it is.

- Parameters:
  - current: The size in use.
  - measured: The content's new measured size.
- Returns: The size to lay out with.

## `UprightUnit/sidewaysWidth`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor let sidewaysWidth: CGFloat?
```

The width the content wraps at while sideways, or `nil` for its natural width.

## `ViewfinderOverlays`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/ViewfinderOverlays.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct ViewfinderOverlays
```

The guides and live aids drawn over the viewfinder: the grid and the level, and, at standard
text sizes, the histogram and the live camera readout (``LiveAidsPanel``).

Everything here is on screen only and never written to the photo (issues #40, #47, #51 and
#52). Nothing takes touches, so the viewfinder's own gestures still work. The guides stay in
clean view; the histogram doesn't, and the readout only when it is pinned.

It tells the model what the live analysis must measure and whether the readout is on, as
the options and the screen change, and turns both off when it goes away (AGENTS.md,
"Battery").

``WindowAppearanceRequest``.

## `WindowAppearanceModifier`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/ScreenAppearance.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct WindowAppearanceModifier
```

Gives the window a screen's appearance: through the app's
``SwiftUICore/EnvironmentValues/windowAppearance`` when it sets one, otherwise with
`preferredColorScheme(_:)`.

SwiftUI applies a window's `preferredColorScheme(_:)` to the whole window scene, which then
no longer shows the system's own setting; the app sets the appearance itself so it can read
that setting for Settings (``SwiftUICore/EnvironmentValues/systemColorScheme``).

public, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/ScreenAppearance.swift`


## `WindowAppearanceRequest/camera(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/ScreenAppearance.swift`

```swift
case camera(ColorScheme?)
```

The camera screen's own appearance: `ColorScheme.dark` for the camera and in night mode,
or `nil` to follow the system (first run, camera access declined, no camera).

## `WindowAppearanceRequest/showsSystemScreen(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/ScreenAppearance.swift`

```swift
case showsSystemScreen(Bool)
```

A sheet or popover that follows the system's light or dark setting (Settings, Presets)
opened (`true`) or closed (`false`). While one is open, the window scene shows the
system's setting, so the app can read and follow it; otherwise the whole scene is dark
with the camera, so the camera's own sheets match the scene they are in.

## `WindowUprightRotation`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeUI/CameraEnvironment.swift`

```swift
@MainActor struct WindowUprightRotation
```

Clears ``SwiftUICore/EnvironmentValues/cameraUprightRotation`` in a window that rotates with
the device, so icons and text aren't turned twice: once by the interface and again as
upright units. The size classes come from the environment, so the same rule covers an iPad
and both displays of a foldable iPhone, and changes as it folds or unfolds.


The content, with the rotation its window needs.

- Parameter content: The content.
